TIER: Threat Implicitness Benchmark for Evaluating LLM Safety Behaviors
Authors: Thu-Hien Trinh-Thi, Hai-Yen Vong, Thanh-Ha Ung-Dung, Tram Ho
First: 2026-09-04T13:13:11+00:00 · Latest: 2026-09-04T13:13:11+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Current LLM safety benchmarks largely rely on binary metrics, overlooking how models respond to harmful prompts with varying threat implicitness. We introduce TIER, a Threat Implicitness Benchmark for behavioral safety evaluation of LLMs. TIER covers four risk domains and four threat levels, from explicit harmful requests to sophisticated jailbreaks. Responses are assessed using a six-label behavior scale and two independent LLM judges. Experiments on six open-weight LLMs show that safety behaviors evolve gradually across threat levels rather than shifting directly from refusal to compliance. Contextual prompts yield the most diverse behaviors, while jailbreaks reveal the largest robustness gaps. Furthermore, models with similar Attack Success Rates can exhibit distinct response distributions, highlighting the need for behavior-aware LLM safety evaluation.
中文标题/摘要
标题：TIER：评估LLM安全行为的威胁隐含性基准
当前LLM安全基准主要依赖于二元指标，忽视了模型对不同威胁隐含性的有害提示的响应。我们引入了TIER，一个用于LLM行为安全评估的威胁隐含性基准。TIER涵盖四个风险领域和四个威胁级别，从明确的有害请求到复杂的越狱。使用六级行为量表和两个独立的LLM评审对响应进行评估。在六个开放权重的LLM上进行的实验表明，安全行为在威胁级别之间逐渐演变，而不是直接从拒绝转变为遵从。上下文提示产生了最丰富的行为，而越狱则揭示了最大的鲁棒性差距。此外，具有相似攻击成功率的模型可能表现出不同的响应分布，突显了对行为意识的LLM安全评估的需求。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the limitations of current large language model (LLM) safety benchmarks, which primarily use binary metrics and fail to account for varying levels of threat implicitness in harmful prompts. Previous methods have not effectively captured the nuances of model responses, leading to an incomplete understanding of LLM safety behaviors. The proposed TIER benchmark introduces a more comprehensive evaluation framework that assesses LLM responses across four risk domains and four threat levels, utilizing a six-label behavior scale and independent judges. This approach is well-motivated as it reveals that safety behaviors evolve gradually rather than transitioning abruptly from refusal to compliance, with contextual prompts generating the most diverse responses. The methodology demonstrates that models can have similar attack success rates yet exhibit different response distributions, emphasizing the necessity for a behavior-aware evaluation of LLM safety, ultimately contributing to a more nuanced understanding of LLM safety behaviors.
本研究解决了当前大型语言模型（LLM）安全基准的局限性，这些基准主要使用二元指标，未能考虑模型对不同威胁隐含程度的有害提示的细微响应。以往的方法未能充分捕捉模型行为的复杂性，导致对安全性如何在不同威胁水平上演变的理解不足。提出的TIER基准引入了一种更全面的评估框架，评估LLM在四个风险领域和四个威胁水平下的响应，利用六标签行为量表和独立评审。该方法的动机明确，因为它揭示了安全行为是逐渐过渡的，而不是通过二元转变，背景提示引发的响应最为多样，而越狱攻击则暴露出显著的鲁棒性差距。在对六个开放权重的LLM进行的实验中，TIER基准有效捕捉了这些复杂性，从而为对LLM安全行为的更细致理解做出了贡献，并支持了对行为意识评估方法的需求。
IndicSafeEval: Safety Robustness of Large Language Models under Multilingual Persuasive Jailbreak Attacks
Authors: Saikat Mondal, Mamta, Deeksha Varshney, Oana Cocarascu, Asif Ekbal
Venue: EMNLP 2026
First: 2026-09-03T12:54:44+00:00 · Latest: 2026-09-04T11:46:25+00:00
Comments: 38 pages, 7 figures, 33 tables. Accepted to Findings of EMNLP 2026. Contains examples of harmful model outputs
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used in multilingual settings, yet their safety is still evaluated primarily in English. This limits our understanding of how alignment failures manifest in low-resource and culturally diverse languages. We introduce IndicSafeEval, a persuasion-based jailbreak evaluation framework for Indian languages. Our benchmark combines ten safety critical content categories with six human-like persuasive strategies across four different Indian languages, such as Hindi, Bengali, Marathi and Punjabi, resulting in 7,200 adversarial prompts. We conduct a systematic black-box evaluation of several open-source LLMs to examine how their safety behaviour varies across languages, persuasion strategies, and risk categories. Our analysis shows that the model does not behave equally safely across all languages and prompt styles. Instead, safety performance depends strongly on both the languages used and the way a request is phrased using persuasive cues. We further observe that different risk categories exhibit different levels of vulnerability, with some types of harmful content being significantly more susceptible to persuasion-based jailbreaks than others. These findings reveal important limitations of current safety evaluations, which are largely English-centric, and underscore the need for multilingual and persuasion-aware benchmarking frameworks to more accurately assess real-world LLM safety. Our implementation is available at https://github.com/MonSaikat/IndicSafeEval. Warning: this paper contains example data that may be offensive or harmful.
中文标题/摘要
标题：IndicSafeEval：多语言说服性越狱攻击下大型语言模型的安全鲁棒性
大型语言模型（LLMs）在多语言环境中的使用日益增加，但其安全性仍主要在英语中进行评估。这限制了我们对低资源和文化多样语言中对齐失败表现的理解。我们引入了IndicSafeEval，这是一个针对印度语言的基于说服的越狱评估框架。我们的基准结合了十个安全关键内容类别和六种类人类的说服策略，涵盖了印地语、孟加拉语、马拉地语和旁遮普语等四种不同的印度语言，共生成7200个对抗性提示。我们对多个开源LLM进行了系统的黑箱评估，以检查它们的安全行为在不同语言、说服策略和风险类别中的变化。我们的分析表明，模型在所有语言和提示风格中并不表现出相同的安全性。相反，安全性能在很大程度上依赖于所使用的语言和请求的措辞方式。我们进一步观察到，不同的风险类别表现出不同的脆弱性，一些有害内容类型对基于说服的越狱攻击显著更易受影响。这些发现揭示了当前安全评估的重要局限性，主要集中在英语，并强调了需要多语言和关注说服的基准框架，以更准确地评估现实世界中的LLM安全性。我们的实现可在https://github.com/MonSaikat/IndicSafeEval获取。警告：本文包含可能冒犯或有害的示例数据。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the limitations of current safety evaluations of large language models (LLMs), which predominantly focus on English, thereby neglecting multilingual contexts and the unique challenges posed by low-resource languages. Previous methods have not adequately captured the nuances of safety in diverse linguistic and cultural settings, leading to a gap in understanding alignment failures. The proposed IndicSafeEval framework introduces a novel evaluation approach that incorporates ten safety-critical content categories and six persuasive strategies across four Indian languages, generating 7,200 adversarial prompts for a comprehensive black-box evaluation of various open-source LLMs. This methodology reveals significant variations in safety performance based on language and persuasion tactics, highlighting the inadequacies of existing English-centric evaluations. The findings emphasize the necessity for multilingual and persuasion-aware frameworks to better assess LLM safety, demonstrating that certain risk categories are more vulnerable to manipulation, thus contributing valuable insights for future research and development in LLM safety assessments.
本研究解决了大型语言模型（LLMs）安全评估的局限性，现有评估主要集中在英语，忽视了多语言环境，尤其是低资源语言。以往的方法未能充分捕捉不同语言和说服策略下的安全性能差异，导致对多样文化背景下的对齐失败缺乏理解。提出的IndicSafeEval框架引入了一个综合评估基准，将安全关键内容类别与四种印度语言中的人类说服策略相结合，生成了7200个对抗性提示。这种方法允许对多种开源LLMs进行系统的黑箱评估，揭示了安全行为在语言和提示措辞上的显著差异。研究结果表明，当前的安全评估不足以涵盖多语言背景，强调了有效评估LLM安全性所需的更具包容性的框架，从而为该领域提供了一个解决这些问题的强大评估工具。
FLY-EVAL++: An Evidence-Driven Evaluation Protocol for Safety-Constrained Flight Prediction with Large Language Models
Authors: Yalun Wu, Junfeng Fang, Jiawei Wang, Haotian Liu, Qijun Yang, Minghan Yang, Hongcheng Guo, Zhoujun Li, Boyang Wang
Venue: Proceedings of the Conference on Language Modeling (COLM), 2026
First: 2026-09-03T16:00:02+00:00 · Latest: 2026-09-03T16:00:02+00:00
Comments: Published as a conference paper at COLM 2026
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Evaluating large language models (LLMs) in safety-critical, physics-governed environments requires more than accuracy-based metrics, because predictions that are numerically close to the ground truth can still violate operational constraints, combine fields in physically inconsistent ways, or fail to produce usable structured outputs. Existing evaluation protocols do not measure these failure modes reliably. We propose FLY-EVAL++, an evidence-driven evaluation protocol that combines deterministic verification of protocol compliance, physical feasibility, and safety constraints with fixed rubric-guided aggregation into interpretable multi-dimensional scores. We instantiate FLY-EVAL++ for Flight Trajectory and Attitude Prediction (FTAP) by extending the PilotBench setting with history-conditioned and multi-step prediction tasks. Across 66 LLMs, safety compliance is the most discriminative dimension of model behavior: models with comparable predictive performance differ by more than 28 points in safety score, and we observe recurrent failures including safety violations under physically plausible predictions and instability in multi-step rollouts. These results show that evaluation in safety-critical domains should measure constraint satisfaction and structured validity explicitly rather than rely on accuracy-centric reporting alone.
中文标题/摘要
标题：FLY-EVAL++：一种基于证据的安全约束飞行预测评估协议
在安全关键、物理约束的环境中评估大型语言模型（LLMs）需要的不仅仅是基于准确性的指标，因为数值上接近真实值的预测仍然可能违反操作约束、以物理不一致的方式组合领域，或未能生成可用的结构化输出。现有的评估协议无法可靠地测量这些失败模式。我们提出了FLY-EVAL++，一种基于证据的评估协议，结合了对协议合规性、物理可行性和安全约束的确定性验证，以及通过固定评分标准引导的聚合，形成可解释的多维评分。我们通过扩展PilotBench设置，结合历史条件和多步预测任务，为飞行轨迹和姿态预测（FTAP）实例化FLY-EVAL++。在66个LLM中，安全合规性是模型行为中最具区分性的维度：具有可比预测性能的模型在安全评分上相差超过28分，我们观察到反复出现的失败，包括在物理上合理的预测下的安全违规和多步展开中的不稳定性。这些结果表明，在安全关键领域的评估应明确测量约束满足和结构有效性，而不是仅依赖于以准确性为中心的报告。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the need for effective evaluation protocols for large language models (LLMs) in safety-critical environments, where traditional accuracy-based metrics fall short by not capturing operational constraints and physical consistency. Existing methods do not reliably assess these failure modes, leading to the development of FLY-EVAL++, an evidence-driven evaluation protocol that integrates deterministic verification of compliance with safety constraints and physical feasibility, along with a rubric-guided aggregation into interpretable scores. This paper contributes by demonstrating that safety compliance is a crucial dimension of model behavior, revealing significant discrepancies in safety scores among models with similar predictive performance. The proposed methodology is applied to Flight Trajectory and Attitude Prediction (FTAP) tasks, where it identifies recurrent failures and emphasizes the importance of measuring constraint satisfaction and structured validity, achieving a more nuanced evaluation of LLMs in safety-critical applications.
本研究关注在安全关键环境中改进大型语言模型（LLMs）评估协议的必要性，因为传统的准确性指标无法捕捉操作约束和物理一致性。现有方法无法可靠地评估这些失败模式，因此开发了FLY-EVAL++，这是一种证据驱动的评估协议，结合了对安全约束和物理可行性的合规性进行确定性验证，以及通过评分标准引导的聚合，形成可解释的分数。本文的贡献在于表明安全合规性是模型行为的一个关键维度，揭示了在预测性能相似的模型之间安全分数存在显著差异。该方法论涉及将FLY-EVAL++应用于飞行轨迹和姿态预测（FTAP）任务，结果表明，即使在合理的预测下也可能发生安全违规，强调了评估应优先考虑约束满足和结构有效性，而不仅仅是准确性。
TACS: Trajectory-Aware Candidate Selection for LLM Jailbreak Suffix Optimization
Authors: Shiliang Xiao
First: 2026-08-30T05:18:28+00:00 · Latest: 2026-09-01T03:40:32+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Gradient-based jailbreak suffix optimization methods typically update the suffix by retaining the candidate with the lowest current loss. We show that this seemingly natural design is fundamentally myopic: candidates that look better under the current-step proxy often fail to produce better jailbreak outcomes later in the search, revealing a form of selection-stage reward hacking. This suggests that candidate selection, rather than candidate generation alone, is a hidden bottleneck in suffix optimization. To address this issue, we propose TACS, a trajectory-aware candidate selection framework for jailbreak suffix optimization. Instead of selecting candidates solely by their immediate loss, TACS augments per-step evaluation with a trajectory-aware proxy and stabilizes selection with reference-policy regularization and a discriminator-estimated chi-squared correction, encouraging choices that remain effective beyond the current step. Experiments on HarmBench show that TACS consistently outperforms strong baselines under the same search budget, substantially improving attack success rates while exhibiting more stable optimization behavior throughout the search. Our findings highlight that mitigating selection-stage reward hacking caused by myopic candidate selection is critical for improving jailbreak suffix optimization.
中文标题/摘要
标题：TACS：用于LLM越狱后缀优化的轨迹感知候选选择
基于梯度的越狱后缀优化方法通常通过保留当前损失最低的候选项来更新后缀。我们表明，这种看似自然的设计在根本上是短视的：在当前步骤代理下看起来更好的候选项往往在搜索后期未能产生更好的越狱结果，揭示了一种选择阶段奖励黑客行为。这表明候选选择，而不仅仅是候选生成，是后缀优化中的一个隐藏瓶颈。为了解决这个问题，我们提出了TACS，一个用于越狱后缀优化的轨迹感知候选选择框架。TACS不仅仅通过候选项的即时损失来选择候选项，而是通过轨迹感知代理增强每一步的评估，并通过参考策略正则化和判别器估计的卡方修正来稳定选择，鼓励在当前步骤之外仍然有效的选择。在HarmBench上的实验表明，TACS在相同搜索预算下始终优于强基线，显著提高了攻击成功率，同时在整个搜索过程中表现出更稳定的优化行为。我们的研究结果强调，减轻由短视候选选择引起的选择阶段奖励黑客行为对于改善越狱后缀优化至关重要。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the limitations of existing gradient-based jailbreak suffix optimization methods, which typically select candidates based solely on their immediate loss, leading to suboptimal long-term outcomes due to a phenomenon known as selection-stage reward hacking. The proposed method, TACS, introduces a trajectory-aware candidate selection framework that enhances the evaluation process by incorporating a trajectory-aware proxy and stabilizing selection through reference-policy regularization and a chi-squared correction estimated by a discriminator. This approach effectively mitigates the issues associated with myopic candidate selection, demonstrating its motivation to improve the overall optimization process. The contributions of the paper are validated through experiments on HarmBench, where TACS significantly outperforms strong baseline methods, achieving higher attack success rates and more stable optimization behavior within the same search budget.
本研究解决了现有基于梯度的越狱后缀优化方法的局限性，这些方法通常仅依赖于根据即时损失选择候选者，导致由于选择阶段奖励黑客行为而产生的次优长期结果。提出的TACS框架引入了一种基于轨迹的候选选择方法，通过结合轨迹感知代理增强评估过程，并通过参考策略正则化和判别器估计的卡方校正来稳定选择。这种方法有效缓解了与短视候选选择相关的问题，贡献了优化性能的提升。在HarmBench上的实验结果表明，TACS在相同搜索预算内显著优于强基线方法，达到了更高的攻击成功率和更稳定的优化行为，从而支持了增强越狱后缀优化的目标。
Validity-Aware Jailbreak Evaluation for Large Language Models
Authors: Qilong Wu, Sahil Wadhwa, Pranab Mohanty, Giri Iyengar, Varun Chandrasekaran
Venue: EMNLP 2026
First: 2026-08-31T23:57:53+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-31T23:57:53+00:00
Comments: To appear on EMNLP 2026 main
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Jailbreak robustness has become central to large language model (LLM) safety evaluation, yet prevailing methodologies rely primarily on refusal behavior, semantic resemblance, and intent-matching heuristics that emphasize linguistic plausibility rather than correctness. We identify a key limitation in existing evaluations: many jailbreak intents depend on instructional validity rather than epistemic factuality, allowing realistic-looking responses to be labeled successful despite being factually or procedurally incorrect. To address this gap, we propose Sequential Epistemic and Action-Level Validation (SEAV), a verification-centric jailbreak evaluation framework that decomposes responses into ordered steps and evaluates both validity and correctness. SEAV combines LLM-as-a-judge mechanisms for semantic interpretation with retrieval-grounded verification using external knowledge sources, assessing whether generated content is factually correct, structurally consistent, and operationally capable of advancing harmful objectives. Empirically, SEAV cuts the false-positive rate on SD-A (a curated strategic-dishonesty diagnostic) by 14.9\,pp vs. the strongest baseline, and reclassifies 22.1\%--51.0\% of sampled prior-labeled successes as invalid across three of four public benchmarks. Together, these results show that enforcing correctness substantially reshapes measured robustness: many previously labeled jailbreak successes are reclassified as invalid, and results are stable across the tested search backends and evaluator models. Code and data are available at https://github.com/Ardor-Wu/SEAV.
中文标题/摘要
标题：针对大型语言模型的有效性感知越狱评估
越狱鲁棒性已成为大型语言模型（LLM）安全评估的核心，但现有方法主要依赖于拒绝行为、语义相似性和意图匹配启发式，这些方法强调语言的合理性而非正确性。我们识别出现有评估的一个关键限制：许多越狱意图依赖于指令有效性而非认知事实性，允许看似真实的响应被标记为成功，尽管在事实或程序上是错误的。为了解决这一问题，我们提出了顺序认知和行动级验证（SEAV），这是一个以验证为中心的越狱评估框架，它将响应分解为有序步骤，并评估有效性和正确性。SEAV结合了LLM作为评判机制的语义解释与使用外部知识源的检索基础验证，评估生成内容是否在事实上正确、结构上连贯，并在操作上能够推进有害目标。实证结果表明，SEAV将SD-A（一个策划的战略不诚实诊断）的假阳性率降低了14.9个百分点，相较于最强基线，并在四个公共基准中的三个重新将22.1%至51.0%的先前标记成功样本重新分类为无效。这些结果表明，强制正确性显著重塑了测量的鲁棒性：许多先前标记的越狱成功被重新分类为无效，且结果在测试的搜索后端和评估模型中保持稳定。代码和数据可在https://github.com/Ardor-Wu/SEAV获取。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical issue of jailbreak robustness in large language models (LLMs), highlighting limitations in existing evaluation methods that prioritize linguistic plausibility over factual correctness. Previous methodologies primarily focused on refusal behavior and intent-matching heuristics, which often misclassify responses as successful despite factual inaccuracies. The proposed Sequential Epistemic and Action-Level Validation (SEAV) framework offers a novel approach by breaking down responses into ordered steps and assessing both validity and correctness, integrating LLM-as-a-judge mechanisms with external knowledge verification. This methodology significantly reduces the false-positive rate on the SD-A diagnostic by 14.9 percentage points compared to the strongest baseline and reclassifies a substantial portion of previously labeled successes as invalid across multiple benchmarks. These findings indicate that emphasizing correctness can dramatically alter the evaluation of jailbreak robustness, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed method in enhancing safety assessments of LLMs.
本研究关注大型语言模型（LLMs）中的越狱鲁棒性问题，强调现有评估方法的局限性，这些方法侧重于语言的合理性而非事实的正确性。以往的方法主要依赖拒绝行为和意图匹配启发式，这常常导致错误地将响应标记为成功，尽管它们在事实上的不准确性。提出的顺序认知和行动级验证（SEAV）框架通过将响应分解为有序步骤并评估有效性和正确性，提供了一种新颖的方法，结合了LLM作为评判机制与基于检索的验证。该方法显著提高了评估准确性，在SD-A诊断中将假阳性率降低了14.9个百分点，并在多个基准测试中将22.1%至51.0%的先前标记为成功的案例重新分类为无效。这些结果表明，优先考虑正确性可以显著改变对LLMs鲁棒性的感知，且在不同评估者和搜索后端中结果一致。
Risk-Adjusted Harm Scoring for Automated Red Teaming for LLMs in Financial Services
Authors: Fabrizio Dimino, Bhaskarjit Sarmah, Stefano Pasquali
First: 2026-03-11T14:14:13+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-31T14:24:11+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Existing LLM safety evaluations rely on binary attack-success rates and domain-agnostic taxonomies, leaving regulated Banking, Financial Services, and Insurance (BFSI) deployments exposed to failures elicited through legally or professionally plausible framing. We introduce RAHS (Risk-Adjusted Harm Score), a risk-sensitive metric jointly capturing disclosure severity, disclaimer mitigation, and inter-judge agreement, and FinRedTeamBench, a 989-prompt benchmark spanning seven BFSI risk areas and 34 sub-categories mapped to regulatory frameworks. Evaluation uses an ensemble of three heterogeneous LLM judges, validated against human experts, and an adaptive multi-turn red-teaming pipeline. On nine open-weight models, RAHS preserves separation under near-ceiling ASR, ranking is stable under hyperparameter sweeps, and multi-turn pressure drives not only more jailbreaks but more operationally severe disclosures, exposing failure modes that single-turn, domain-agnostic evaluations cannot reveal.
中文标题/摘要
标题：针对金融服务中大型语言模型的自动红队风险调整危害评分
现有的大型语言模型安全评估依赖于二元攻击成功率和领域无关的分类法，使受监管的银行、金融服务和保险（BFSI）部署面临通过合法或专业上合理的框架引发的失败。我们引入了RAHS（风险调整危害评分），这是一种风险敏感的指标，联合捕捉披露严重性、免责声明缓解和评审者间一致性，以及FinRedTeamBench，这是一个涵盖七个BFSI风险领域和34个子类别的989个提示基准，映射到监管框架。评估使用三位异构大型语言模型评审者的集成，经过与人类专家的验证，以及自适应多轮红队流程。在九个开放权重模型上，RAHS在接近上限的攻击成功率下保持分离，排名在超参数搜索中稳定，多轮压力不仅驱动更多越狱行为，还导致更具操作严重性的披露，暴露出单轮、领域无关评估无法揭示的失败模式。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the limitations of existing safety evaluations for large language models (LLMs) in the Banking, Financial Services, and Insurance (BFSI) sectors, which often rely on binary attack-success rates and generic taxonomies, leaving these systems vulnerable to nuanced failures. The proposed approach, Risk-Adjusted Harm Score (RAHS), introduces a risk-sensitive metric that evaluates disclosure severity, disclaimer mitigation, and inter-judge agreement, alongside a comprehensive benchmark called FinRedTeamBench, which includes 989 prompts across various BFSI risk areas. This method improves upon past evaluations by utilizing an ensemble of three diverse LLM judges and an adaptive multi-turn red-teaming pipeline, effectively revealing failure modes that traditional single-turn assessments miss. The paper contributes a robust framework for assessing LLM safety in regulated environments, demonstrating that RAHS maintains performance stability across different models and hyperparameters, while also uncovering more severe operational risks through multi-turn interactions.
本研究解决了现有大型语言模型（LLMs）在银行、金融服务和保险（BFSI）领域的安全评估不足之处，这些评估通常依赖于二元成功率和通用分类法，使得这些系统容易受到微妙失败的影响。以往的方法未能充分捕捉该领域风险的复杂性，因此提出了风险调整危害评分（RAHS），该指标评估披露严重性和评审者间一致性，同时考虑监管框架。本文的贡献在于引入RAHS和FinRedTeamBench，这是一个涵盖多个BFSI风险领域的989个提示的综合基准。该方法论涉及使用三种不同的LLM评审者和自适应多轮红队测试管道，证明RAHS能够有效识别比传统单轮评估更严重的操作失败，在多个模型和超参数设置中实现稳定排名，从而支持提高受监管环境中LLM安全性的目标。
Do We Still Need Humans in the Loop? Human vs. LLM Annotation in Active Learning for TikTok Hate Speech Detection
Authors: Ahmad Dawar Hakimi, Lea Hirlimann, Isabelle Augenstein, Hinrich Schütze
First: 2026-04-15T14:10:58+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-31T11:56:04+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Annotating data remains a costly bottleneck for supervised NLP. Active learning (AL) reduces the number of human labels needed by selecting only the most informative instances, while instruction-tuned LLMs attack the same bottleneck from the other side, making labels cheap enough to annotate entire corpora. This raises two questions: can LLM labels replace human labels within the AL loop, and does AL remain necessary when entire corpora can be cheaply labeled? We investigate both by training supervised hate speech classifiers on a new dataset of 278K German political TikTok comments, comparing human and LLM annotation under matched conditions. LLM annotation at scale outperforms human-supervised classifiers at roughly one-tenth the cost, for both a closed-source (GPT-5.2) and an open-weight (Qwen3.5-122B-A10B) LLM, and the advantage is robust under soft-label evaluation. It hinges on the annotation interface: only a two-question decomposition mirroring the human annotation task unlocks it. AL provides no reliable advantage over random sampling in our prefiltered pool. Error structure depends on the LLM: only GPT-5.2 matches the human FP/FN balance, while other variants over-flag border-control and economic-competition discourse. Humans remain essential as evaluators; for training labels, the question shifts to which LLM, which interface, and what shape of pool.
中文标题/摘要
标题：我们还需要人类参与吗？在TikTok仇恨言论检测中人类与LLM注释的主动学习比较
数据注释仍然是监督NLP的一个成本瓶颈。主动学习（AL）通过仅选择最具信息量的实例来减少所需的人类标签数量，而经过指令调优的LLM则从另一个角度解决同一瓶颈，使标签变得足够便宜以注释整个语料库。这引发了两个问题：LLM标签能否替代AL循环中的人类标签，以及当整个语料库可以被廉价标注时，AL是否仍然必要？我们通过在一个包含278K条德国政治TikTok评论的新数据集上训练监督仇恨言论分类器来研究这两个问题，比较在匹配条件下的人类和LLM注释。大规模LLM注释的表现优于人类监督的分类器，成本约为十分之一，适用于封闭源（GPT-5.2）和开放权重（Qwen3.5-122B-A10B）LLM，并且在软标签评估下优势稳健。这依赖于注释接口：只有与人类注释任务相对应的两个问题的分解才能解锁它。在我们预过滤的池中，AL对随机抽样没有可靠的优势。错误结构依赖于LLM：只有GPT-5.2匹配人类的FP/FN平衡，而其他变体则过度标记边境控制和经济竞争话语。人类作为评估者仍然至关重要；对于训练标签，问题转向哪个LLM、哪个接口以及什么形状的池。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of costly data annotation in supervised natural language processing (NLP), particularly in the context of hate speech detection on TikTok. Previous methods relied heavily on human annotation, which is time-consuming and expensive, while recent advancements in instruction-tuned large language models (LLMs) have made it possible to generate labels at a lower cost. This study proposes to evaluate whether LLM-generated labels can effectively replace human labels in the active learning (AL) loop and whether AL is still necessary when LLMs can label entire datasets cheaply. The methodology involves training hate speech classifiers on a dataset of 278,000 German political TikTok comments, comparing the performance of classifiers using human versus LLM annotations. The findings indicate that LLM annotation significantly outperforms human annotation at a fraction of the cost, with the performance being robust across different LLMs, although the effectiveness depends on the annotation interface used. The paper concludes that while LLMs can replace human labels for training, human evaluators remain crucial for assessing model performance.

From Detection to Refusal: Safer LLMs via Circuit-Guided Weight Scaling
Authors: Kuan-Lin Chu, Chung-En Sun, Tsui-Wei Weng
Venue: EMNLP 2026
First: 2026-08-30T08:01:42+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-30T08:01:42+00:00
Comments: Accepted to Findings of the 2026 Conference on Empirical Methods in Natural Language Processing (EMNLP 2026)
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Despite extensive alignment efforts, Large Language Models (LLMs) remain vulnerable to generating unsafe content under adversarial prompting, yet the internal mechanisms by which safety behaviors are implemented remain poorly understood. We study LLM safety from a mechanistic interpretability perspective and characterize a multi-stage *safety circuit* that organizes refusal behavior, consisting of (i) $\textbf{Harmful Detection Heads}$ that respond to harmful inputs, (ii) $\textbf{Safety Neurons}$ that mediate and stabilize safety signals in the residual stream, and (iii) $\textbf{Refusal Heads}$ that translate these signals into safe response generation. Using targeted attention-head and neuron-level interventions, we provide causal evidence consistent with this circuit organization, showing that suppressing upstream Harmful Detection Heads disrupts downstream refusal behavior and that safety neurons mediate this interaction. We validate that this decomposition recurs across multiple LLM architectures and adversarial attack settings, and use simple, architecture-preserving weight scaling as a mechanistic probe to test its functional relevance. Across six LLMs, circuit-guided scaling improves safety rates under attacks by 26.5%, while incurring only a 1.7% accuracy drop across four standard benchmarks. Overall, our results support a circuit-level interpretation of LLM safety and suggest that mechanistic abstractions can reveal stable and transferable patterns underlying aligned behavior.
中文标题/摘要
标题：从检测到拒绝：通过电路引导的权重缩放实现更安全的LLM
尽管进行了广泛的对齐努力，大型语言模型（LLM）在对抗性提示下仍然容易生成不安全内容，但实现安全行为的内部机制仍然不够清楚。我们从机制可解释性的角度研究LLM安全性，并描述一个多阶段的*安全电路*，该电路组织拒绝行为，包括（i）响应有害输入的$\textbf{有害检测头}$，（ii）在残差流中介导和稳定安全信号的$\textbf{安全神经元}$，以及（iii）将这些信号转化为安全响应生成的$\textbf{拒绝头}$。通过针对性注意力头和神经元级干预，我们提供了与该电路组织一致的因果证据，表明抑制上游有害检测头会干扰下游拒绝行为，而安全神经元介导了这种互动。我们验证了这种分解在多个LLM架构和对抗攻击设置中反复出现，并使用简单的、保持架构的权重缩放作为机制探针来测试其功能相关性。在六个LLM中，电路引导的缩放在攻击下提高了26.5%的安全率，同时在四个标准基准上仅造成1.7%的准确率下降。总体而言，我们的结果支持LLM安全性的电路级解释，并表明机制抽象可以揭示对齐行为背后的稳定和可转移模式。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the persistent vulnerability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to generating unsafe content despite alignment efforts, highlighting the need for a better understanding of the internal mechanisms that govern safety behaviors. Previous methods have focused on alignment but often fail to provide insights into the underlying processes, leading to inadequate safety measures. The proposed approach introduces a multi-stage safety circuit that includes Harmful Detection Heads, Safety Neurons, and Refusal Heads, which collectively enhance the model's ability to refuse harmful prompts. This method is well-motivated as it offers a mechanistic interpretability perspective, allowing for targeted interventions that demonstrate causal relationships within the model's architecture. The research methodology involves using circuit-guided weight scaling across six LLMs, resulting in a 26.5% improvement in safety rates under adversarial attacks with only a 1.7% drop in accuracy on standard benchmarks, thus supporting the goal of enhancing LLM safety while maintaining performance.

Turning the Spell Around: Lightweight Alignment Amplification via Rank-One Safety Injection
Authors: Harethah Abu Shairah, Hasan Abed Al Kader Hammoud, George Turkiyyah, Bernard Ghanem
Venue: EMNLP 2026
First: 2025-08-28T13:22:33+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-30T07:43:32+00:00
Comments: EMNLP 2026, Main Conference
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety alignment in Large Language Models (LLMs) often involves mediating internal representations to refuse harmful requests. Recent research has demonstrated that these safety mechanisms can be bypassed by ablating or removing specific representational directions within the model. In this paper, we propose the opposite approach: Rank-One Safety Injection (ROSI), a white-box method that amplifies a model's safety alignment by permanently steering its activations toward the refusal-mediating subspace. ROSI operates as a simple, fine-tuning-free rank-one weight modification applied to all residual stream write matrices. The required safety direction can be computed from a small set of harmful and harmless instruction pairs. We show that ROSI consistently increases safety refusal rates - as evaluated by Llama Guard 3 - while preserving the utility of the model on standard benchmarks such as MMLU, HellaSwag, and Arc. Furthermore, we show that ROSI can also re-align 'uncensored' models by amplifying their own latent safety directions, demonstrating its utility as an effective last-mile safety procedure. Our results suggest that targeted, interpretable weight steering is a cheap and potent mechanism to improve LLM safety, complementing more resource-intensive fine-tuning paradigms.
中文标题/摘要
标题：扭转咒语：通过秩一安全注入实现轻量级对齐放大
大型语言模型（LLMs）中的安全对齐通常涉及调解内部表示以拒绝有害请求。最近的研究表明，这些安全机制可以通过消除或移除模型内特定的表示方向来绕过。在本文中，我们提出了相反的方法：秩一安全注入（ROSI），这是一种白盒方法，通过永久性地将模型的激活引导到拒绝调解子空间来放大模型的安全对齐。ROSI作为一种简单的、无需微调的秩一权重修改，应用于所有残差流写矩阵。所需的安全方向可以从一小组有害和无害的指令对中计算得出。我们展示了ROSI在Llama Guard 3的评估中始终提高了安全拒绝率，同时在MMLU、HellaSwag和Arc等标准基准上保持了模型的效用。此外，我们还展示了ROSI可以通过放大自身潜在安全方向来重新对齐“未审查”模型，证明其作为有效的最后一公里安全程序的实用性。我们的结果表明，针对性的、可解释的权重引导是一种廉价而有效的机制，可以提高LLM的安全性，补充更资源密集的微调范式。
Summary / 总结
This paper addresses the challenge of safety alignment in Large Language Models (LLMs), where existing methods often fail due to the ability of harmful requests to bypass safety mechanisms by altering internal representations. The proposed approach, Rank-One Safety Injection (ROSI), differs from traditional methods by amplifying safety alignment through a straightforward, fine-tuning-free modification of weight matrices, steering model activations toward a subspace that enhances refusal of harmful requests. The contribution of this research lies in demonstrating that ROSI can effectively increase safety refusal rates while maintaining model performance on standard benchmarks like MMLU, HellaSwag, and Arc. The methodology involves calculating the necessary safety direction from a limited set of harmful and harmless instruction pairs, and the results indicate that ROSI serves as a cost-effective and interpretable solution for improving LLM safety, even for uncensored models.

Single Canonical Prompts Underestimate LLM Safety's Surface-Form Sensitivity
Authors: Yongxi Zhou, Junwei Yao, Yuanzhe Liu, Zihan Dong, Wenbo Ye, Jiaxi Wen, Lai Yun Choi
First: 2026-08-01T22:28:31+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-30T05:09:19+00:00
Comments: Accepted at the Sci-FM Workshop @ COLM 2026 (non-archival). Workshop version with reviews: https://openreview.net/forum?id=mZh0MqpOOC
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
A benchmark score is a measurement instrument, yet most benchmarks read each item at a single canonical surface form. We ask whether that reading is faithful: when an item's intent is held fixed and only its meaning-preserving surface form varies, does the canonical-form score estimate model behavior well, and how much of any variation is decoding/judge noise rather than signal? We instantiate this in safety, a high-stakes setting with no gold label to average toward. To avoid prior confounds, we pre-author the reformulations (refusal-free, mostly non-LLM: machine back-translation and a Matrix-Language-Frame code-switch generator) so an identical surface form reaches every model, score all responses with one human-anchored, vendor-neutral judge (Claude, kappa = 0.86 vs. human on unsafe compliance, stable across languages, cross-checked by GPT-4o), and verify intent preservation. On 370 seeds x 5 surface forms x 5 models, no single transformation is uniformly most dangerous (6 of 20 per-transformation McNemar tests survive correction, most protective). Yet evaluating only the canonical prompt underestimates unsafe compliance: the union of unsafe outcomes across forms exceeds even the worst single form by 3.3-12.9 pp, with bootstrap 95% CIs excluding zero for all five models, and 5-13% of seeds safe on canonical are unsafe under some reformulation -- above a zero stochasticity floor (canonical resampled five times at temperature 0 gives 0/370 new exposures). The size of this gap is model-dependent (largest on Gemini 2.5 Pro). One form recovers only ~53% of a model's observed unsafe surface and about three reach 85% -- a redundancy characterization of this form set, not of a defined population. A benign control (XSTest) suggests the instability is bidirectional, though the benign and harmful pools are not item-matched. We release the dataset, code, and per-response labels.
中文标题/摘要
标题：单一规范提示低估了大型语言模型安全性的表面形式敏感性
基准分数是一种测量工具，但大多数基准以单一规范表面形式读取每个项目。我们询问这种读取是否忠实：当一个项目的意图保持不变，仅其保持意义的表面形式变化时，规范形式分数是否能很好地估计模型行为，以及任何变化中有多少是解码/评判噪声而非信号？我们在安全性这一高风险环境中实例化这一点，因其没有金标准可供平均。为了避免先前的混淆，我们预先授权了重构（无拒绝，主要非大型语言模型：机器回译和矩阵语言框架代码切换生成器），以便相同的表面形式到达每个模型，使用一个人类锚定的、中立的评审（Claude，kappa = 0.86与人类在不安全合规性上的比较，在各语言中稳定，由GPT-4o交叉验证），并验证意图保持。在370个种子x 5个表面形式x 5个模型中，没有单一变换是普遍最危险的（20个每变换的McNemar测试中有6个在校正后存活，大多数是保护性的）。然而，仅评估规范提示低估了不安全合规性：跨形式的不安全结果的并集甚至超过了最糟糕的单一形式3.3-12.9个百分点，所有五个模型的自助法95%置信区间均不包括零，且5-13%的在规范下安全的种子在某些重构下是不安全的——高于零随机性底线（规范在温度0下重采样五次给出0/370个新暴露）。这一差距的大小依赖于模型（在Gemini 2.5 Pro上最大）。一种形式仅恢复了模型观察到的不安全表面的约53%，大约三种形式达到了85%——这是对这一形式集的冗余特征，而非定义人口的特征。一个良性对照（XSTest）表明不稳定性是双向的，尽管良性和有害池并未按项目匹配。我们发布了数据集、代码和每个响应的标签。
Summary / 总结
This research investigates the effectiveness of benchmark scores in evaluating large language models (LLMs) in terms of safety, particularly questioning whether single canonical surface forms accurately reflect model behavior when the intent remains constant. Previous methods relied on a single canonical prompt, which may not capture the full range of model responses, leading to an underestimation of unsafe compliance. The proposed approach involves pre-authoring multiple reformulations of prompts and evaluating them with a consistent human-anchored judge to ensure intent preservation, addressing the limitations of prior methods. The study contributes a comprehensive dataset and analysis demonstrating that evaluating only canonical prompts significantly underestimates unsafe outcomes, with findings showing that the union of unsafe results across various surface forms exceeds the worst single form by a notable margin. The methodology involved testing 370 seeds across five surface forms and five models, revealing that no single transformation is uniformly the most dangerous, and performance metrics indicate that a substantial portion of seeds deemed safe under canonical prompts are unsafe under alternative formulations, thus supporting the need for a more nuanced evaluation of LLM safety.
本研究解决了在安全背景下评估大型语言模型（LLMs）时现有基准分数的局限性，单一规范提示可能无法准确反映模型行为，因为其对表面形式的敏感性。以往的方法通常依赖于单一表面形式，这可能导致对模型合规性和安全性的误导性评估。提出的方法涉及预先编写多个提示的改写，并使用一致的人类评估者对响应进行评分，以确保不同表面形式之间的意图保持。研究发现，仅评估规范提示会低估不安全合规性，显示不同形式的组合不安全结果超过最差单一形式的幅度显著。该方法论表明，没有单一的转化形式是普遍最危险的，研究结果强调了安全评估的模型依赖性，为高风险场景中LLM行为的深入理解做出了贡献。
NeuronFuzz: Safety Neuron Guided Fuzzing for LLM Safety Evaluation
Authors: Zhiyuan Xu, Muhammad Firhard Roslan, Joseph Gardiner, Sana Belguith, Lichao Wu
First: 2026-08-26T12:44:32+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-26T12:44:32+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety evaluation is critical for assessing whether aligned Large Language Models (LLMs) remain robust against jailbreak attacks. Existing automated testing methods, however, largely rely on response-level feedback: each candidate prompt typically requires generating a target-model response to evaluate its attack effectiveness. This process is expensive and, more importantly, provides only sparse guidance on strongly aligned models, where most candidates are rejected with the same failure outcome. This paper presents NeuronFuzz, a white-box fuzzing framework that exploits internal safety neurons as continuous execution feedback for LLM safety evaluation. A SafetyOracle converts safety-neuron activations into a continuous safety alarm score that serves as feedback for fuzzing and can be obtained during prefill, eliminating response generation from the fuzzing loop. To construct the SafetyOracle, NeuronFuzz uses template-invariant harmful and benign inputs and stability-aware selection to identify a compact set of safety neurons whose activations capture harmful-intent recognition. Moreover, since the safety alarm score is differentiable, NeuronFuzz uses its gradients to identify safety-sensitive template positions and a masked language model to generate fluent, context-compatible mutations while preserving original harmful payload and avoiding additional optimization variables. We evaluate NeuronFuzz across 21 text and multimodal models. Across five white-box source models, it achieves a 76-100% jailbreak discovery rate, outperforming baselines by up to 48 percentage points. Its optimized templates further transfer zero-shot to open-weight and six proprietary target models, achieving average ASR and top-5 ensemble ASR (EASR) of 69.6%/92.6% and 44.1%/60.0%, respectively.
中文标题/摘要
标题：NeuronFuzz：安全神经元引导的LLM安全评估模糊测试
安全评估对于评估对齐的大型语言模型（LLM）是否能够抵御越狱攻击至关重要。然而，现有的自动化测试方法主要依赖于响应级反馈：每个候选提示通常需要生成目标模型的响应以评估其攻击有效性。这个过程成本高昂，更重要的是，对于大多数候选者在相同失败结果下被拒绝时，仅提供稀疏的指导。 本文提出了NeuronFuzz，一个白盒模糊测试框架，利用内部安全神经元作为LLM安全评估的连续执行反馈。SafetyOracle将安全神经元激活转换为连续的安全警报分数，作为模糊测试的反馈，并且可以在预填充期间获得，从而消除模糊测试循环中的响应生成。为了构建SafetyOracle，NeuronFuzz使用模板不变的有害和良性输入以及稳定性感知选择，识别出一组紧凑的安全神经元，其激活捕捉有害意图识别。此外，由于安全警报分数是可微分的，NeuronFuzz利用其梯度识别安全敏感的模板位置，并使用掩码语言模型生成流畅、上下文兼容的变异，同时保留原始有害负载并避免额外的优化变量。我们在21个文本和多模态模型上评估了NeuronFuzz。在五个白盒源模型中，它实现了76-100%的越狱发现率，超越基线最多48个百分点。其优化模板进一步零-shot转移到开放权重和六个专有目标模型，分别实现69.6%/92.6%和44.1%/60.0%的平均ASR和前5集成ASR（EASR）。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical need for effective safety evaluation of aligned Large Language Models (LLMs) against jailbreak attacks, highlighting limitations in existing automated testing methods that rely on response-level feedback, which is costly and provides limited guidance. The proposed NeuronFuzz framework differs by utilizing internal safety neurons for continuous execution feedback, thereby eliminating the need for response generation in the fuzzing process. This approach is well-motivated as it aims to enhance the robustness of LLMs against harmful intents. NeuronFuzz constructs a SafetyOracle that translates safety-neuron activations into a continuous safety alarm score, allowing for efficient fuzzing. The methodology is evaluated across 21 text and multimodal models, achieving a jailbreak discovery rate of 76-100% and outperforming existing methods by up to 48 percentage points, demonstrating its effectiveness in enhancing LLM safety evaluation.
本研究解决了对齐的大型语言模型（LLMs）在评估其抵御越狱攻击的安全性方面的迫切需求，强调了现有依赖响应级反馈的自动化测试方法的局限性，这些方法成本高且对强对齐模型提供有限指导。提出的NeuronFuzz框架通过利用内部安全神经元提供连续执行反馈，从而消除了模糊处理过程中对响应生成的需求，展现出其独特性。这种方法具有良好的动机，因为它提高了安全评估的效率和有效性。NeuronFuzz构建了一个SafetyOracle，将安全神经元的激活转化为连续的安全警报分数，从而能够识别安全敏感的模板位置并生成上下文兼容的变异。该方法在21个文本和多模态模型上进行了评估，达到了76-100%的越狱发现率，并且显著超越现有方法，提升幅度高达48个百分点，从而证明其支持稳健安全评估的能力。
'Ghaib in Translation' aka Unseen Harm: Measuring Cross-Script Safety Inconsistency with 'Missed-in-Urdu' Scores in LLM Hate Speech Detection
Authors: Fawzia Zehra, Kara-Isitt, Sonal Khosla, Stephen Swift
First: 2026-08-25T07:58:40+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-25T07:58:40+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Urdu, the world's tenth most spoken language with 246 million speakers, remains almost entirely absent from mainstream LLM safety evaluation and nine years of WOAH proceedings. To investigate whether this absence has measurable consequences for content moderation reliability, five large language models, GPT-4o, Claude Sonnet 4.5, Gemini 2.5 Flash, Qwen-2.5, and Llama-3.1, were tested across six datasets spanning Nastaliq Urdu, Roman Urdu, English, and code-switched Urdu-English. Across the five Urdu-script datasets, label instability between original-script and English-translation classification ranged from 15.9% (Gemini 2.5 Flash) to 31.6% (Qwen-2.5), with a 'Missed-in-Urdu' rate, content flagged as harmful in English translation but passed as normal in the original script, ranging from 2.4% to 9.9% (median 4.3%). A complete enumeration of all 205 papers across nine ALW/WOAH editions via the ACL Anthology API confirms zero dedicated Urdu papers across the entire period. Results indicate that current LLMs provide uneven safety assurance across Urdu's script varieties, with smaller open-weight models showing substantially higher instability and missed-harm rates than frontier closed models.
中文标题/摘要
标题：'翻译中的隐秘'即未见的危害：使用'乌尔都语遗漏'分数测量跨脚本安全不一致性在LLM仇恨言论检测中的应用
乌尔都语是全球第十大语言，拥有2.46亿说话者，但在主流LLM安全评估和九年的WOAH会议中几乎完全缺席。为了调查这种缺席是否对内容审核的可靠性产生可测量的影响，测试了五个大型语言模型：GPT-4o、Claude Sonnet 4.5、Gemini 2.5 Flash、Qwen-2.5和Llama-3.1，涵盖了六个数据集，包括Nastaliq乌尔都语、罗马乌尔都语、英语和代码切换的乌尔都语-英语。在五个乌尔都语脚本数据集中，原始脚本与英语翻译分类之间的标签不稳定性范围为15.9%（Gemini 2.5 Flash）到31.6%（Qwen-2.5），'乌尔都语遗漏'率，即在英语翻译中标记为有害但在原始脚本中通过的内容，范围为2.4%到9.9%（中位数4.3%）。通过ACL Anthology API对九个ALW/WOAH版本中205篇论文的完整枚举确认，在整个期间内没有专门的乌尔都语论文。结果表明，当前的LLM在乌尔都语的脚本变体中提供的不均匀安全保障，较小的开放权重模型显示出显著更高的不稳定性和遗漏危害率，超过了前沿的封闭模型。

Previous methods have failed to adequately evaluate the safety of content in different scripts, leading to inconsistencies in harm detection. This study proposes a novel approach by measuring 'Missed-in-Urdu' scores to quantify the discrepancies in hate speech detection across various Urdu scripts and English translations. ADVERSA: Measuring Multi-Turn Guardrail Degradation and Judge Reliability in Large Language Models
Authors: Harry Owiredu-Ashley
Venue: 2026 IEEE/ACIS 24th International Conference on Software Engineering Research, Management and Applications (SERA), Towson, MD, USA, 2026, pp. 414-417
First: 2026-03-10T03:00:34+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-25T05:47:14+00:00
Comments: 12 pages, 12 figures. Independent research. Code and artifacts: https://github.com/Harry-Ashley/adversa-guardrail-degradation. Published in the 2026 IEEE/ACIS 24th International Conference on Software Engineering Research, Management and Applications (SERA), pp. 414-417. Recipient of the Best Paper Award at IEEE/ACIS SERA 2026
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Most adversarial evaluations of large language model (LLM) safety assess single prompts and report binary pass/fail outcomes, which fails to capture how safety properties evolve under sustained adversarial interaction. We present ADVERSA, an automated red-teaming framework that measures guardrail degradation dynamics as continuous per-round compliance trajectories rather than discrete jailbreak events. ADVERSA uses a fine-tuned 70B attacker model (ADVERSA-Red, Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct with QLoRA) that eliminates the attacker-side safety refusals that render off-the-shelf models unreliable as attackers, scoring victim responses on a structured 5-point rubric that treats partial compliance as a distinct measurable state. We report a controlled experiment across three frontier victim models (Claude Opus 4.6, Gemini 3.1 Pro, GPT-5.2) using a triple-judge consensus architecture in which judge reliability is measured as a first-class research outcome rather than assumed. Across 15 conversations of up to 10 adversarial rounds, we observe a 26.7% jailbreak rate with an average jailbreak round of 1.25, suggesting that in this evaluation setting, successful jailbreaks were concentrated in early rounds rather than accumulating through sustained pressure. We document inter-judge agreement rates, self-judge scoring tendencies, attacker drift as a failure mode in fine-tuned attackers deployed out of their training distribution, and attacker refusals as a previously-underreported confound in victim resistance measurement. All limitations are stated explicitly. Attack prompts are withheld per responsible disclosure policy; all other experimental artifacts are released.
中文标题/摘要
标题：ADVERSA：测量大型语言模型中的多轮护栏退化和评审可靠性
大多数对大型语言模型（LLM）安全性的对抗性评估评估单个提示并报告二元通过/失败结果，这未能捕捉安全属性在持续对抗交互下的演变。我们提出了ADVERSA，一个自动化红队框架，测量护栏退化动态，作为连续的每轮合规轨迹，而不是离散的越狱事件。ADVERSA使用经过微调的70B攻击者模型（ADVERSA-Red，Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct与QLoRA），消除了攻击者侧的安全拒绝，使现成模型在攻击者中不可靠，使用结构化的5分制评分受害者响应，将部分合规视为一个独特的可测量状态。我们报告了在三个前沿受害者模型（Claude Opus 4.6，Gemini 3.1 Pro，GPT-5.2）中进行的受控实验，使用三重评审共识架构，其中评审可靠性被视为一项一流的研究结果，而不是假设。在15次对话中，最多进行10轮对抗，我们观察到26.7%的越狱率，平均越狱轮次为1.25，表明在该评估环境中，成功的越狱集中在早期轮次，而不是通过持续压力累积。我们记录了评审间一致性率、自我评审评分倾向、在超出其训练分布的微调攻击者中作为失败模式的攻击者漂移，以及作为受害者抵抗测量中先前未报告的混淆因素的攻击者拒绝。所有限制均明确说明。攻击提示根据负责任的披露政策被保留；所有其他实验材料均已发布。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the limitations of existing adversarial evaluations of large language models (LLMs), which typically assess safety through single prompts and binary outcomes, failing to capture the dynamics of safety properties during prolonged adversarial interactions. The proposed ADVERSA framework introduces a continuous measurement of guardrail degradation using a fine-tuned 70B attacker model, which enhances reliability by eliminating safety refusals that compromise off-the-shelf models. The study contributes by employing a triple-judge consensus architecture to measure judge reliability as a primary outcome, revealing a 26.7% jailbreak rate across 15 conversations with an average of 1.25 jailbreak rounds, indicating that vulnerabilities are more prevalent in initial interactions. This methodology allows for a nuanced understanding of compliance trajectories and highlights critical factors such as inter-judge agreement and attacker drift, ultimately supporting the goal of improving LLM safety assessments.

NeuronGuard: Robust LLM Safety Alignment via Ablation-Aware Safety Signal Redistribution
Authors: Anjun Gao, Yueyang Quan, Yufei Xia, Zhuqing Liu, Minghong Fang
Venue: EMNLP 2026
First: 2026-08-25T01:36:54+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-25T01:36:54+00:00
Comments: To appear in EMNLP 2026 (Findings)
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety alignment in large language models (LLMs) remains brittle against a growing spectrum of attacks. Jailbreak attacks bypass safety mechanisms through crafted prompts, while neuron-level attacks directly prune safety-critical neurons post-deployment. Both exploit a common weakness: safety-relevant information concentrates in a sparse neuron subset. We present NeuronGuard, a fine-tuning-stage defense that simultaneously hardens LLMs against both attack classes by redistributing safety signals across a broader set of neurons. NeuronGuard dynamically identifies safety-critical neurons via periodically refreshed per-layer linear classifiers, forces refusal behavior under deliberate neuron ablation, and applies KL-divergence regularization for distributional consistency. A randomized gradient projection strategy preserves downstream task utility by resolving conflicts between the defense and task objectives. We provide a formal guarantee that NeuronGuard strictly reduces the attack success rate (ASR) upper bound, and experiments across three LLMs, six state-of-the-art attack strategies, and multimodal settings confirm near-zero ASR while maintaining task accuracy, including against white-box adaptive adversaries.
中文标题/摘要
标题：NeuronGuard：通过消融感知安全信号重分配实现稳健的LLM安全对齐
大型语言模型（LLMs）的安全对齐在面对日益增长的攻击谱时仍然脆弱。越狱攻击通过精心设计的提示绕过安全机制，而神经元级攻击则在部署后直接修剪安全关键神经元。两者都利用了一个共同的弱点：安全相关信息集中在稀疏的神经元子集中。我们提出了NeuronGuard，这是一种微调阶段的防御方法，通过在更广泛的神经元集上重新分配安全信号，来同时增强LLMs对这两类攻击的抵御能力。NeuronGuard通过定期更新的每层线性分类器动态识别安全关键神经元，在故意消融神经元时强制拒绝行为，并应用KL散度正则化以保持分布一致性。一种随机梯度投影策略通过解决防御与任务目标之间的冲突，保持下游任务的效用。我们提供了一个正式保证，NeuronGuard严格降低攻击成功率（ASR）上限，并且在三种LLM、六种最先进的攻击策略和多模态设置下的实验确认了接近零的ASR，同时保持任务准确性，包括对抗白盒自适应对手的情况。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the vulnerability of large language models (LLMs) to various safety attacks, specifically jailbreak and neuron-level attacks, which exploit the concentration of safety-relevant information in a limited number of neurons. Previous methods have struggled to provide robust defenses against these threats, leading to a need for a more effective approach. The proposed method, NeuronGuard, enhances safety alignment by redistributing safety signals across a wider array of neurons, employing dynamic identification of safety-critical neurons and incorporating KL-divergence regularization to maintain consistency. This approach not only mitigates the risk of attack but also preserves the utility of LLMs for downstream tasks. Experimental results demonstrate that NeuronGuard achieves near-zero attack success rates while maintaining task accuracy across multiple LLMs and attack strategies, effectively supporting its goals of improving safety alignment in LLMs.
本研究解决了大型语言模型（LLMs）在面对各种安全攻击时的脆弱性，特别是越狱攻击和神经元级攻击，这些攻击利用了安全相关信息集中在有限神经元中的弱点。以往的方法在提供对这些威胁的强大防御方面存在困难，因此需要一种更全面的方法。提出的方法NeuronGuard通过在更广泛的神经元中重新分配安全信号来增强安全对齐，利用动态识别安全关键神经元并应用KL散度正则化以保持一致性。这种方法不仅降低了攻击成功率，还保持了LLMs在下游任务中的效用。实验结果表明，NeuronGuard在多个LLMs和攻击策略中实现了近零的攻击成功率，同时保持了任务准确性，有效支持其强大安全对齐的目标。
Register Shifts Break LLM Safety: A Bengali Benchmark with Culturally Grounded Harms
Authors: Naymul Islam, Nusrat Jahan Lia, Shubhashis Roy Dipta, Sabik Bin Sultan, Abdullah Khan Zehady
First: 2026-08-23T10:09:00+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-23T10:09:00+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Bengali is the seventh-most-spoken language globally, yet LLM safety evaluation remains overwhelmingly English-centric. We introduce BanglaSafe, a benchmark of 879 Bengali prompts combining 309 natively authored prompts with 570 expert-reviewed prompts, spanning 17 culturally grounded harm categories and five prompting conditions that vary language, writing style, and authority framing. Evaluating 18 frontier LLMs, we find that over half of all responses are unsafe or partially unsafe (53.6%) while 14.7% contains strictly harmful content, and that the strongest observed effect is not the switch from English to Bengali but the choice of writing style within Bengali: the same harmful request phrased as a formal newspaper investigation succeeds 17 percentage points more often than the same request phrased as a casual message, with no adversarial engineering involved. We further show that existing safety classifiers struggle to reliably evaluate Bengali content, with even frontier models failing on nearly half of all cases.
中文标题/摘要
标题：注册班次打破LLM安全：一个具有文化基础危害的孟加拉基准
孟加拉语是全球第七大语言，但LLM安全评估仍然以英语为中心。我们推出了BanglaSafe，这是一个包含879个孟加拉提示的基准，结合了309个本土创作的提示和570个专家审查的提示，涵盖17个文化基础的危害类别和五种不同的提示条件，变化包括语言、写作风格和权威框架。评估18个前沿LLM时，我们发现超过一半的响应是不安全或部分不安全的（53.6%），而14.7%的内容包含严格有害的信息，观察到的最强效应不是从英语切换到孟加拉语，而是在孟加拉语中选择的写作风格：同样的有害请求以正式的报纸调查形式表达时，成功率比以随意消息形式表达时高出17个百分点，且没有涉及对抗性工程。我们进一步表明，现有的安全分类器在可靠评估孟加拉内容方面存在困难，甚至前沿模型在近一半的案例中也未能成功。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the lack of safety evaluation for large language models (LLMs) in Bengali, despite it being the seventh-most-spoken language globally, highlighting the English-centric focus of existing evaluations. Previous methods have primarily evaluated LLMs in English, leading to inadequate assessments of Bengali content, particularly regarding culturally grounded harms. The proposed approach, BanglaSafe, introduces a benchmark of 879 Bengali prompts that include both natively authored and expert-reviewed prompts across various harm categories and prompting conditions, effectively addressing the limitations of prior evaluations. This paper contributes by demonstrating that over half of the responses from 18 evaluated LLMs are unsafe or partially unsafe, with a significant finding that the writing style in Bengali influences safety outcomes more than the language switch itself. The methodology involves a comprehensive evaluation of LLM responses to culturally relevant prompts, revealing that existing safety classifiers are insufficient for Bengali content, as they fail to accurately assess nearly half of the cases.

Redteaming Leading Arabic LLMs with ASAS
Authors: Fidaa Abed, Haidar Khan, M Saiful Bari, Babar Khan, Abdalghani Abujabal
First: 2026-08-22T14:34:08+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-22T14:34:08+00:00
Comments: 13 pages, 5 figues, 3 tables
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
As the adoption of large language models (LLMs) grows in Arabic-speaking regions, ensuring their safety and cultural alignment is increasingly critical. However, Arabic LLM safety remains underexplored, especially in adversarial evaluation settings. We introduce the Arabic Safety Index (ASAS), the first fully human-curated Arabic benchmark for redteaming LLMs. ASAS contains 801 prompts spanning 8 safety categories and 8 attack strategies, with ideal responses in Modern Standard Arabic (MSA). We conduct a redteaming evaluation across seven leading models with Arabic capabilities, including GPT-4o, Claude 3.7 Sonnet, and regional models such as ALLaM and FANAR. Human annotators rate responses using a structured 4-point safety scale, revealing that most models fail to defend against 50% of unsafe prompts. Our findings highlight major safety gaps in high-harm categories such as weapons and illicit substances, with direct and obfuscation-based attacks proving most effective. The results also show that language alignment does not readily transfer across languages, and that automated safety judges (e.g., GPT-4o) perform poorly compared to human annotators. ASAS provides a culturally grounded benchmark and redteaming protocol to drive progress in Arabic LLM safety.

随着大型语言模型（LLMs）在阿拉伯语地区的普及，确保其安全性和文化一致性变得愈加重要。然而，阿拉伯语LLM的安全性仍然未得到充分探索，特别是在对抗性评估环境中。我们引入了阿拉伯安全指数（ASAS），这是第一个完全由人工策划的阿拉伯基准，用于对LLMs进行红队测试。ASAS包含801个提示，涵盖8个安全类别和8种攻击策略，并提供现代标准阿拉伯语（MSA）的理想响应。我们对七个具有阿拉伯语能力的领先模型进行了红队评估，包括GPT-4o、Claude 3.7 Sonnet以及区域模型如ALLaM和FANAR。人类注释者使用结构化的4分安全评分标准对响应进行评分，结果显示大多数模型未能抵御50%的不安全提示。我们的发现突显了在武器和非法物质等高危类别中的主要安全缺口，直接攻击和混淆攻击被证明是最有效的。结果还表明，语言一致性并不能轻易跨语言转移，自动安全评估者（例如GPT-4o）的表现远不及人类注释者。ASAS提供了一个文化基础的基准和红队测试协议，以推动阿拉伯语LLM安全性的进展。

Previous methods lacked a comprehensive benchmark for assessing Arabic LLM safety, leading to significant gaps in understanding their vulnerabilities. CLEAR: Continuous Latent Adapter Routing for Utility-Preserving LLM Safety Alignment
Authors: Chengxiao Wang, Enyi Jiang, Xiaojing Liao, Sanmi Koyejo
First: 2026-08-21T16:36:10+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-21T16:36:10+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Improving the safety of large language models (LLMs) often comes at the expense of utility, as globally applied safety tuning may affect model responses to both harmful and benign inputs. We propose \textbf{C}ontinuous \textbf{L}at\textbf{E}nt \textbf{A}dapter \textbf{R}outing (CLEAR), a conditional safety adaptation framework that uses a lightweight hidden-state gate to continuously control the activation strength of a safety low-rank adapter. CLEAR aims to reduce harmful completions while avoiding unnecessary changes to the frozen backbone that could degrade performance on benign prompts. Experiments on widely used safety and utility benchmarks show that CLEAR improves robustness on HarmBench while reducing the utility degradation observed with globally applied safety tuning such as SFT or standard low-rank adaptation (LoRA). On Llama-3-8B-Instruct, CLEAR reduces HarmBench ASR from 32.3\% to 0.5\%, while retaining most of the base model's utility and achieving up to 7.1 percentage points higher GSM8K accuracy than globally applied SFT or LoRA. These results suggest that CLEAR is a promising mechanism for improving the safety--utility trade-off in LLM alignment.
中文标题/摘要
标题：CLEAR：用于保持效用的LLM安全对齐的连续潜在适配器路由
提高大型语言模型（LLMs）的安全性往往会以效用为代价，因为全局应用的安全调优可能会影响模型对有害和良性输入的响应。我们提出了\textbf{C}ontinuous \textbf{L}at\textbf{E}nt \textbf{A}dapter \textbf{R}outing（CLEAR），这是一种条件安全适应框架，使用轻量级的隐藏状态门控来持续控制安全低秩适配器的激活强度。CLEAR旨在减少有害的完成，同时避免对冻结主干的不必要更改，这可能会降低对良性提示的性能。对广泛使用的安全和效用基准的实验表明，CLEAR在HarmBench上提高了鲁棒性，同时减少了全局应用安全调优（如SFT或标准低秩适配器（LoRA））所观察到的效用下降。在Llama-3-8B-Instruct上，CLEAR将HarmBench ASR从32.3\%降低到0.5\%，同时保留了大部分基础模型的效用，并在GSM8K准确率上比全局应用的SFT或LoRA高出多达7.1个百分点。这些结果表明，CLEAR是改善LLM对齐中安全与效用权衡的有希望的机制。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of enhancing the safety of large language models (LLMs) without compromising their utility, as traditional safety tuning methods often lead to a decline in model performance on benign inputs. Previous approaches, such as globally applied safety tuning and standard low-rank adaptation (LoRA), have shown limitations by negatively impacting the model's responses. The proposed Continuous Latent Adapter Routing (CLEAR) framework introduces a lightweight hidden-state gate that allows for continuous control over the activation strength of a safety adapter, effectively mitigating harmful outputs while preserving the model's utility. This method is well-motivated as it seeks to balance safety and utility in LLM alignment. Experimental results demonstrate that CLEAR significantly reduces harmful completions on the HarmBench benchmark from 32.3% to 0.5% while achieving improved performance on the GSM8K task, with an increase of up to 7.1 percentage points in accuracy compared to traditional methods, indicating its effectiveness in addressing the safety-utility trade-off.
本研究解决了在不影响大型语言模型（LLM）效用的情况下提高其安全性的问题，因为传统方法如全局应用的安全调优可能会对模型在良性输入上的表现产生负面影响。现有方法，如标准微调（SFT）和低秩适应（LoRA），往往导致显著的效用下降，因此需要更有效的解决方案。提出的连续潜在适配器路由（CLEAR）框架引入了一种轻量级的隐藏状态门，以动态控制安全低秩适配器的激活，从而在减少有害输出的同时保持模型在良性提示上的性能。该方法通过使用已建立的安全性和效用基准进行评估，结果表明CLEAR在HarmBench上显著减少了有害完成，并提高了GSM8K的准确性，使HarmBench ASR从32.3%降至0.5%，并在准确性上显著超过传统方法。这些发现表明CLEAR有效地平衡了LLM对齐中的安全性和效用。
RouteScan: A Non-Intrusive Approach to Auditing MoE LLMs Safety via Expert Routing Telemetry
Authors: Bo Lv, Zhiheng Xu, KeDong Xiu, Ruyi Ding, Tianhang Zheng, Zhibo Wang, Kui Ren
First: 2026-05-24T02:06:44+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-21T12:55:57+00:00
Comments: 11 pages. Revised manuscript with expanded experiments
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
As Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architectures are increasingly adopted for scaling Large Language Models (LLMs), safety auditing becomes necessary to verify whether these models produce or facilitate harmful behaviors during operation. However, existing content-based auditing methods typically require access to user prompts, model internals, or outputs, potentially exposing sensitive user information and creating a tension between LLM safety and user privacy. On the other hand, we observe that, in MoE models, different inputs induce different sparse expert-routing patterns, which produce measurable footprints in low-level GPU execution telemetry. We refer to these hardware-observable signals induced by expert-routing decisions as expert routing telemetry; they are derived from GPU execution rather than from router logits or token-level routing assignments. Inspired by this observation, we propose RouteScan, a non-intrusive auditing framework for detecting harmful behaviors through such routing-induced GPU telemetry. Specifically, RouteScan utilizes the number of active GPU threads allocated to expert modules during the prefilling phase as a discriminative micro-architectural fingerprint, and builds a lightweight detection pipeline that isolates cross-domain invariant risk indicators for the precise identification of malicious prompts. Comprehensive evaluations on four open-source MoE LLMs with distinct routing designs demonstrate that RouteScan achieves strong generalization, with an AUROC exceeding 0.91 on unseen harmful domains. Moreover, privacy stress tests show that, although aggregated execution telemetry retains input-related attribute information, full prompts and exact sensitive fields cannot be reliably recovered under the evaluated attacks.

标题：RouteScan：一种通过专家路由遥测对MoE LLM安全性进行非侵入式审计的方法
随着混合专家（MoE）架构在大规模语言模型（LLM）中的日益采用，安全审计变得必要，以验证这些模型在操作过程中是否产生或促进有害行为。然而，现有的基于内容的审计方法通常需要访问用户提示、模型内部或输出，可能会暴露敏感用户信息，并在LLM安全性与用户隐私之间产生紧张关系。另一方面，我们观察到，在MoE模型中，不同的输入会引发不同的稀疏专家路由模式，这在低级GPU执行遥测中产生可测量的足迹。我们将这些由专家路由决策引发的硬件可观察信号称为专家路由遥测；它们源自GPU执行，而非路由器logits或令牌级路由分配。受此观察启发，我们提出了RouteScan，一种通过这种路由引发的GPU遥测检测有害行为的非侵入式审计框架。具体而言，RouteScan利用在预填充阶段分配给专家模块的活跃GPU线程数量作为区分性微架构指纹，并构建一个轻量级检测管道，隔离跨域不变风险指标，以精确识别恶意提示。对四个具有不同路由设计的开源MoE LLM的全面评估表明，RouteScan实现了强大的泛化能力，在未见过的有害领域中AUROC超过0.91。此外，隐私压力测试表明，尽管聚合的执行遥测保留了与输入相关的属性信息，但在评估的攻击下，无法可靠地恢复完整提示和确切的敏感字段。

Certified Multi-Turn Robustness for LLM Safety via Compositional Bounds and Safety Persistence
Authors: Yang Liu, Bin Chong, Wenkai Yang, Shuai Zhang, Yancheng Chen, Feiyu Han, GuoZhen, Cheng Zhang, Huaibing Xie, Changze Lv, Shihan Dou, Pluto Zhou
First: 2026-08-21T07:43:06+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-21T07:43:06+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) are vulnerable to multi-turn jailbreak attacks that progressively manipulate conversation context. Existing certified robustness methods are limited to single-turn inputs; naive multi-turn composition yields bounds that degrade exponentially in the number of turns. We introduce Multi-Turn Certified Robustness (MTCR), a framework that models conversational safety via State-Adversarial MDPs and defines $k$-turn certified robustness as the worst-case safety probability across $k$ adversarial turns. MTCR comprises: (i) compositional certification via embedding-space mode decomposition, yielding tighter certified lower bounds than naive multiplication; (ii) $(α,β)$-safety persistence, improving the degradation rate from $\underline{p}^{k}$ to $β^k$ (with $β> \underline{p}$) and yielding interpretable horizon estimates; (iii) matching information-theoretic upper bounds establishing tightness; and (iv) a unified algorithm combining these results. Experiments on six LLMs under $ε$-bounded and Crescendo-style attacks confirm that empirical safety consistently exceeds the certified bounds.
中文标题/摘要
标题：通过组合界限和安全持久性实现LLM安全的认证多轮鲁棒性
大型语言模型（LLMs）易受多轮越狱攻击，这些攻击逐步操控对话上下文。现有的认证鲁棒性方法仅限于单轮输入；简单的多轮组合导致界限在轮数上呈指数级下降。我们引入了多轮认证鲁棒性（MTCR），这是一个通过状态对抗MDP建模对话安全的框架，并将$k$轮认证鲁棒性定义为$k$轮对抗中的最坏情况安全概率。MTCR包括：（i）通过嵌入空间模式分解进行组合认证，产生比简单乘法更紧的认证下界；（ii）$(α,β)$-安全持久性，将降级率从$\underline{p}^{k}$改善到$β^k$（其中$β> \underline{p}$），并产生可解释的时间估计；（iii）匹配信息论上界，建立紧性；（iv）结合这些结果的统一算法。在$ε$-有界和Crescendo风格攻击下对六个LLM的实验确认，经验安全性始终超过认证界限。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the vulnerability of large language models (LLMs) to multi-turn jailbreak attacks that exploit conversation context, highlighting the limitations of existing certified robustness methods that only apply to single-turn inputs and suffer from exponential degradation in multi-turn scenarios. The proposed Multi-Turn Certified Robustness (MTCR) framework distinguishes itself by employing State-Adversarial Markov Decision Processes (MDPs) to define $k$-turn certified robustness, which improves upon previous methods by offering tighter certified lower bounds through compositional certification and enhancing safety persistence to mitigate degradation rates. This paper contributes a comprehensive approach that integrates these advancements into a unified algorithm, demonstrating its effectiveness through experiments on six LLMs, where the empirical safety consistently surpasses the certified bounds under various adversarial attacks, thereby supporting the goal of enhancing conversational safety. Calibrate-Then-Delegate: Safety Monitoring with Risk and Budget Guarantees via Model Cascades
Authors: Edoardo Pona, Milad Kazemi, Mehran Hosseini, Yali Du, David Watson, Osvaldo Simeone, Nicola Paoletti
First: 2026-04-15T11:05:59+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-21T07:17:49+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Monitoring LLM safety at scale requires balancing cost and accuracy: a cheap latent-space probe can screen every input, but hard cases should be escalated to a more expensive expert. Existing cascades delegate based on probe uncertainty, but uncertainty is a poor proxy for the utility of an expert call, as it ignores whether the expert would actually improve the prediction. To address this problem, we introduce Calibrate-Then-Delegate (CTD), a model-cascade approach that provides probabilistic guarantees on either computation cost or safety performance while enabling instance-level (streaming) decisions. CTD builds on a novel delegation value (DV) probe, a lightweight model operating on the same internal representations as the safety probe that predicts the benefit of replacing the probe score with the expert score after an expert call. CTD calibrates a threshold on the DV signal using held-out data and multiple hypothesis testing, yielding finite-sample guarantees while optimising the complementary objective. Evaluated on four safety datasets using learned-attention probes, CTD improves over uncertainty-based delegation over much of the budget range, avoids unnecessary expert calls, and extracts positive-value subsets even from groups where the expert score is worse than the probe score on average. Ablations across four probe architectures show that these findings are not specific to any particular architecture.
中文标题/摘要
标题：校准后委派：通过模型级联实现风险和预算保证的安全监测
大规模监测LLM安全性需要平衡成本和准确性：便宜的潜在空间探测器可以筛选每个输入，但困难案例应升级到更昂贵的专家。现有的级联基于探测器的不确定性进行委派，但不确定性并不是专家调用效用的良好代理，因为它忽略了专家是否真的会改善预测。为了解决这个问题，我们引入了校准后委派（CTD），这是一种模型级联方法，提供计算成本或安全性能的概率保证，同时支持实例级（流式）决策。CTD基于一种新颖的委派价值（DV）探测器，该轻量级模型在与安全探测器相同的内部表示上运行，预测在专家调用后用专家分数替代探测器分数的好处。CTD使用保留数据和多重假设检验对DV信号进行阈值校准，提供有限样本保证，同时优化互补目标。在四个安全数据集上使用学习注意力探测器进行评估，CTD在大部分预算范围内优于基于不确定性的委派，避免了不必要的专家调用，并从专家分数平均低于探测器分数的组中提取出正值子集。对四种探测器架构的消融实验表明，这些发现并不特定于任何特定架构。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of monitoring the safety of large language models (LLMs) at scale, where a balance between cost and accuracy is crucial. Previous methods relied on probe uncertainty to delegate tasks, which proved inadequate as it did not account for whether expert intervention would actually enhance predictions. The proposed Calibrate-Then-Delegate (CTD) approach introduces a novel delegation value (DV) probe that assesses the potential benefit of expert calls, allowing for instance-level decisions while providing probabilistic guarantees on computation cost or safety performance. This methodology was evaluated on four safety datasets and demonstrated improved performance over uncertainty-based delegation, effectively reducing unnecessary expert calls and identifying valuable subsets even when expert scores were lower than probe scores on average, thus supporting the goals of efficient and accurate safety monitoring.
本研究解决了大规模监控大型语言模型（LLMs）安全性的问题，其中成本和准确性之间的平衡至关重要。以往的方法依赖于探测器的不确定性进行任务委派，但这种方法不足以考虑专家干预的实际效用。提出的Calibrate-Then-Delegate（CTD）方法引入了一种模型级联方法，结合了一种新颖的委派值（DV）探测器，以预测专家干预的好处，从而提供对计算成本或安全性能的概率保证。该方法允许实例级决策，并在四个安全数据集上进行了评估，显示出相较于基于不确定性的委派方法的改进，减少了不必要的专家调用，并有效识别出即使在专家评分平均低于探测器评分的情况下也具有价值的子集，从而支持其增强安全监控的目标。
aiXamine: Unified Black-Box Evaluation of Cross-Dimensional Trade-offs in LLM Safety, Security, and Privacy
Authors: Fatih Deniz, Yazan Boshmaf, Dorde Popovic, Issa Khalil
First: 2026-08-20T20:33:35+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-20T20:33:35+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
The critical failure modes in deployed large language models (LLMs) are cross-dimensional: a model can score 99.3 in safety alignment while refusing one in three benign queries, or improve across every capability metric while losing 21 points in privacy. Existing evaluation frameworks that assess safety, security, and privacy independently cannot detect these patterns. We introduce aiXamine, a unified black-box platform that evaluates LLM trustworthiness across safety, security, and privacy as interdependent properties. aiXamine orchestrates 46 tests across nine services through an automated red-teaming pipeline, producing hierarchical risk profiles, from prompt-level diagnostics to cross-service trade-off analytics, that enable reproducible comparison of proprietary and open-weight systems under identical conditions. Applying aiXamine to over 120 LLMs through more than 5,000 test runs, we conduct the largest joint safety, security, and privacy study to date and uncover three cross-dimensional phenomena invisible to single-axis evaluation. First, safety enforcement incurs a quantifiable safety tax: stronger alignment systematically increases over-refusal, forcing providers to choose between protection and utility. Second, privacy is near-orthogonal to other trustworthiness dimensions and not captured by standard alignment. Third, we identify and formally characterize distillation-induced robustness collapse: off-policy distillation without on-policy correction causes entropy collapse, catastrophically destroying robustness (56.9$\to$2.6) on the same base architecture. These findings, compounded by diminishing returns from scale and category-dependent safety behaviors, demonstrate that trustworthiness is inherently multi-dimensional: progress along one axis does not guarantee, and can actively undermine, progress along others, yet current alignment methods treat it as a single objective.
中文标题/摘要
标题：aiXamine：统一黑箱评估大型语言模型安全性、保密性和隐私中的跨维权衡
已部署的大型语言模型（LLMs）中的关键失效模式是跨维的：一个模型在安全对齐上得分99.3，但拒绝三分之一的良性查询，或者在每个能力指标上都有所提升，但隐私损失21分。现有的评估框架独立评估安全性、保密性和隐私，无法检测这些模式。我们引入了aiXamine，一个统一的黑箱平台，评估LLM在安全性、保密性和隐私方面的可信度，作为相互依赖的属性。aiXamine通过自动化红队管道协调46个测试，涵盖九项服务，生成从提示级诊断到跨服务权衡分析的分层风险概况，使在相同条件下对专有和开放权重系统的可重复比较成为可能。通过超过5000次测试运行将aiXamine应用于120多个LLM，我们进行了迄今为止最大的联合安全性、保密性和隐私研究，揭示了三种在单轴评估中不可见的跨维现象。首先，安全执行会产生可量化的安全税：更强的对齐系统性地增加过度拒绝，迫使提供者在保护和效用之间做出选择。其次，隐私与其他可信度维度几乎正交，标准对齐无法捕捉。第三，我们识别并正式表征了蒸馏引起的鲁棒性崩溃：没有政策校正的离政策蒸馏导致熵崩溃，灾难性地破坏了鲁棒性（56.9$\to$2.6），在相同的基础架构上。这些发现，加上规模的递减收益和类别依赖的安全行为，表明可信度本质上是多维的：在一个轴上的进展并不保证，并且可能会积极破坏其他轴上的进展，而当前的对齐方法将其视为单一目标。
Summary / 总结
This article addresses the critical failure modes in large language models (LLMs), which often exhibit interdependent issues in safety, security, and privacy that existing evaluation frameworks fail to capture due to their independent assessments. Previous methods inadequately evaluate these dimensions, leading to a lack of understanding of their trade-offs, while the proposed aiXamine platform offers a unified black-box evaluation approach that assesses these properties as interconnected. The contribution of this paper lies in its introduction of aiXamine, which conducts 46 tests across nine services, producing comprehensive risk profiles and enabling reproducible comparisons of LLMs under identical conditions. The methodology involves automated red-teaming to analyze over 120 LLMs through more than 5,000 test runs, revealing significant findings such as the safety tax, the near-orthogonality of privacy to other dimensions, and the detrimental effects of distillation-induced robustness collapse. These results highlight the multi-dimensional nature of trustworthiness in LLMs, demonstrating that improvements in one area can negatively impact others, thus challenging the current single-objective alignment methods.
本研究关注大型语言模型（LLMs）中的关键失效模式，这些模式在安全性、安全性和隐私方面往往表现出相互依赖的问题，而现有的评估框架由于独立评估而无法捕捉到这些问题。以往的方法无法充分评估这些维度，导致对涉及的权衡缺乏理解，而提出的aiXamine平台则提供了一种统一的黑箱评估，能够共同评估这些属性。该方法具有良好的动机，因为它揭示了可能危及模型可信度的复杂交互。该方法论包括一个自动化的红队测试管道，进行46项测试，涵盖九项服务，生成分层风险概况，并实现LLMs的可重复比较。将aiXamine应用于120多个LLMs并进行超过5000次测试运行，得出了重要发现，包括识别安全税和蒸馏引起的鲁棒性崩溃，表明在一个维度上的改进可能会对其他维度产生不利影响，从而强调了LLMs可信度的多维特性。
Enforcing LLM Safety through DMD-based Classification of Prompt-Response Embedding Dynamics
Authors: Mohamed Akrout, Olivera Kotevska, Dan Wilson
First: 2026-08-20T02:50:44+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-20T02:50:44+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed in high-stakes applications, yet their tendency to generate toxic, harmful, or policy-violating content poses significant risks. Detecting these unsafe outputs efficiently in a black-box manner remains an open challenge. In this paper, we extend a recently proposed dynamical systems framework designed for hallucination detection to LLM safety classification. By projecting both prompts and responses into high-dimensional embedding spaces and fitting separate Koopman-based predictive models for safe and unsafe regimes, we classify new outputs using a new differential residual score that compares prediction errors of the safe and unsafe regimes. A key contribution is the incorporation of the prompt and response embedding dynamics, yielding fitted Koopman operators that capture crucial interaction patterns. We evaluate our black-box method across three safety benchmarks using three embedding models. Our results show that incorporating prompt embeddings yields consistent improvements, particularly for interaction-dependent violations when paired with causal decoders (e.g., in Llama-3), while response-only violations benefit more from dense semantic embedding representations. These findings opens the door for using dynamical systems to analyze AI systems rather than the dominant paradigm of using AI to model dynamical systems.
中文标题/摘要
标题：通过基于DMD的提示-响应嵌入动态分类来强化大型语言模型的安全性
大型语言模型（LLMs）越来越多地应用于高风险场景，但它们生成有毒、有害或违反政策的内容的倾向带来了重大风险。以黑箱方式高效检测这些不安全输出仍然是一个未解决的挑战。本文扩展了最近提出的用于幻觉检测的动态系统框架，以用于LLM安全分类。通过将提示和响应投影到高维嵌入空间，并为安全和不安全状态拟合单独的基于Koopman的预测模型，我们使用新的微分残差分数对新输出进行分类，该分数比较安全和不安全状态的预测误差。一个关键贡献是将提示和响应嵌入动态纳入其中，得到了捕捉关键交互模式的拟合Koopman算子。我们在三个安全基准上使用三种嵌入模型评估了我们的黑箱方法。我们的结果表明，结合提示嵌入可以带来一致的改进，特别是在与因果解码器（例如Llama-3）配对时，针对交互依赖的违规行为，而仅响应的违规行为则更受益于密集语义嵌入表示。这些发现为使用动态系统分析AI系统打开了大门，而不是使用AI建模动态系统的主流范式。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the growing concern of Large Language Models (LLMs) generating unsafe content in high-stakes applications, highlighting the challenge of efficiently detecting such outputs in a black-box manner. Previous methods have struggled with this task, often lacking the ability to effectively classify outputs based on their safety. This paper proposes a novel approach that extends a dynamical systems framework for hallucination detection to classify LLM safety by utilizing high-dimensional embedding spaces and fitting separate Koopman-based predictive models for safe and unsafe outputs. The methodology incorporates prompt and response embedding dynamics to capture interaction patterns, leading to improved classification performance. The proposed method is evaluated across three safety benchmarks, demonstrating consistent improvements, particularly in interaction-dependent violations, thus supporting the goal of enhancing LLM safety classification.

Fair ASR: Re-Evaluating Black-Box Jailbreaks under Shared Target-Call Budgets
Authors: Zhida He, Xiaoyu Wen, Han Qi, Ziyuan Zhou, Peng Yu, Jiajia Li, Chaochao Lu, Qiaosheng Zhang
First: 2026-08-18T04:26:39+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-18T04:26:39+00:00
Comments: 29 pages, 8 figures, 13 tables. Code: https://github.com/xsddys/Fair-ASR
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Reliable jailbreak evaluation is essential for assessing LLM safety, but most existing studies rely solely on attack success rate (ASR) without accounting for its dependence on attack budgets, resulting in unfair comparisons across methods. Existing compute-aware evaluations reduce heterogeneous resources into FLOPs, which is difficult to estimate for black-box models and fails to capture resource-specific constraints. To provide a comparable evaluation basis, we introduce Fair-ASR, an evaluation protocol for black-box jailbreak attacks under shared target-call budgets B, using target calls as a directly observable and method-agnostic comparison axis while tracking attacker calls separately for efficiency analysis. We re-evaluate 11 representative attacks under the Fair-ASR protocol and find that attack rankings change substantially across target-call budgets, simple stochastic perturbations and hand-crafted templates remain highly competitive under equal target access, and no evaluated LLM-driven method is efficient in both target and attacker calls. Motivated by this efficiency gap, we introduce ReCode, a compositional budget-efficient attack that combines desensitization rewriting with two effective low-cost primitives identified by Fair-ASR. Under a budget of 20 target calls, ReCode achieves 85% ASR on GPT-5 while requiring only 7.19 attacker calls per request on average, showing strong efficiency in both target and attacker calls.
中文标题/摘要
标题：公平ASR：在共享目标调用预算下重新评估黑箱越狱
可靠的越狱评估对于评估大型语言模型（LLM）的安全性至关重要，但现有大多数研究仅依赖攻击成功率（ASR），而未考虑其对攻击预算的依赖，导致方法之间的不公平比较。现有的计算感知评估将异构资源简化为FLOPs，这对于黑箱模型难以估计，并未捕捉资源特定的约束。为了提供可比较的评估基础，我们引入了公平ASR，这是一种在共享目标调用预算B下评估黑箱越狱攻击的评估协议，使用目标调用作为直接可观察和方法无关的比较轴，同时单独跟踪攻击者调用以进行效率分析。我们在公平ASR协议下重新评估了11个代表性攻击，发现攻击排名在目标调用预算之间发生了显著变化，简单的随机扰动和手工模板在平等目标访问下仍然具有高度竞争力，并且没有评估的LLM驱动方法在目标和攻击者调用中都有效率。受到这一效率差距的启发，我们引入了ReCode，这是一种组合预算高效的攻击，结合了去敏感重写和公平ASR识别的两种有效低成本原语。在20个目标调用的预算下，ReCode在GPT-5上实现了85%的ASR，同时平均每个请求仅需7.19个攻击者调用，显示出在目标和攻击者调用中的强效率。

Making Your LLMs More Objective: Stabilizing LLM Safety Behavior Across Traits with Trait-Invariant Safety Tuning
Authors: Lang Cao
First: 2026-08-12T06:33:55+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-12T06:33:55+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Aligned large language models (LLMs) are expected to exhibit safety behavior based on the content of the user request: they should refuse unsafe requests and comply with safe ones. However, we show that the same request can elicit substantially different safety decisions under different traits assigned in the system prompt, a failure mode we call trait-induced safety variation. To measure this failure, we introduce refusal-based metrics: Trait-Induced Deviation measures dataset-level deviation from the no-trait baseline, while Trait-Induced Flip Rate measures whether the same request receives different safety decisions across traits. We then provide a representation-level analysis of the mechanism behind trait-induced safety shifts and find that traits perturb the model's safety representations within a low-dimensional subspace. To achieve trait-invariant safety, where safety behavior remains stable across traits, we introduce Trait-Invariant Safety Tuning (TIST), a simple yet effective self-distillation framework that aligns an LLM's trait-conditioned behavior with its no-trait behavior. Guided by our analysis, we further propose Trait-Subspace Neutralization (TraSN), an instantiation of TIST, which enforces invariance only within the identified trait subspace. Experiments show that TraSN improves trait-invariant safety and strengthens harmful-request safety while preserving general capability. Our results highlight traits as an important factor in LLM safety and robust model behavior.
中文标题/摘要
标题：提高您的大型语言模型的客观性：通过特征不变安全调优稳定大型语言模型的安全行为
对齐的大型语言模型（LLMs）预计会根据用户请求的内容表现出安全行为：它们应该拒绝不安全的请求并遵守安全的请求。然而，我们展示了同一请求在系统提示中分配的不同特征下可能引发显著不同的安全决策，这种失败模式我们称之为特征引起的安全变异。为了衡量这种失败，我们引入了基于拒绝的指标：特征引起的偏差衡量数据集级别的偏差与无特征基线的偏差，而特征引起的翻转率衡量同一请求在不同特征下是否收到不同的安全决策。然后，我们提供了特征引起的安全变化机制的表示级分析，发现特征在低维子空间内扰动模型的安全表示。为了实现特征不变安全，即安全行为在不同特征间保持稳定，我们引入了特征不变安全调优（TIST），这是一种简单而有效的自蒸馏框架，将LLM的特征条件行为与其无特征行为对齐。在我们的分析指导下，我们进一步提出了特征子空间中和（TraSN），这是TIST的一种实例，它仅在识别的特征子空间内强制不变性。实验表明，TraSN提高了特征不变安全性，并增强了对有害请求的安全性，同时保持了整体能力。我们的结果强调特征是LLM安全性和稳健模型行为的重要因素。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the issue of inconsistent safety behavior in aligned large language models (LLMs), where the same request can lead to different safety decisions based on the traits assigned in the system prompt, a phenomenon termed trait-induced safety variation. Previous methods did not adequately account for this variability, leading to unreliable safety responses. The proposed approach, Trait-Invariant Safety Tuning (TIST), aims to stabilize safety behavior across traits by aligning the model's trait-conditioned behavior with its no-trait behavior through a self-distillation framework. The paper contributes by introducing refusal-based metrics to quantify safety deviations and by proposing Trait-Subspace Neutralization (TraSN) to enforce safety invariance within a specific trait subspace. Experimental results demonstrate that TraSN enhances trait-invariant safety and improves responses to harmful requests while maintaining overall model capabilities, thus supporting the goal of consistent safety behavior in LLMs.
